and then directly applies an operation
\{\mathsf{I},\mathsf{X},\mathsf{Y},\mathsf{Z}\}
.
The gadget is
C_{\mathsf{g}}
with every gate replaced by a gadget from
Section

7.3
.
For
\mathsf{g}\in\{\mathsf{I}(A),\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A),\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}}(A),\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A)\}
,
C_{\mathsf{g}}
is the corresponding circuit from
\mathsf{CSSTRANSVERSAL}
.
The gadgets are padded with error correction gadgets
\mathsf{EC}^{{\mathsf{qLDPC}({i})}}
(
Section

6.3
) such that they are of uniform size.

\blacktriangleleft

Proof.

Set
\epsilon_{*,\mathsf{GATE}}=\min(\epsilon_{*,\mathsf{qLDPCTRANSVERSAL}},\epsilon_{*,\mathsf{qLDPC~PREP}})
.
The width and depth bounds follow from the respective gadgets
The weight enumerator bounds follow from having composed a constant number of gadgets, each with weight enumerator bounded as
\mathsf{poly}(d_{i})e^{-\beta d_{i}}
on
[0,\epsilon_{*,\mathsf{GATE}}]
, and from the depth bound (for padding).
∎

{theorem}

[Constant space overhead threshold theorem]
There exists a constant
\epsilon_{*}\in(0,1)
such that, for any Clifford+T circuit
C
with classical input and classical output of width
W
, depth
D
, and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
satisfying
45
45

45

This condition rules out exponentially deep circuits but is otherwise not very restrictive.

W\geq\log^{7}\left(\frac{WD}{\epsilon}\right)
, there exists a circuit
\overline{C}
that is a fault-tolerant gadget for
C
with trivial input and output and bad fault paths
\mathcal{F}
.
Let
V=\frac{WD}{\epsilon}
.
Then,
\overline{C}
has width
\overline{W}
and depth
\overline{D}
satisfying the bounds

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=O(W)

(7.91)

\displaystyle\overline{D}

\displaystyle=O(D\log^{8.91}(V)\mathrm{polyloglog}(V))~.

(7.92)

On
x\in[0,\epsilon_{*}]
, the weight enumerator of
\mathcal{F}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}{\left(\mathcal{F};~x\right)}\leq\epsilon~.

(7.93)

Construction.
Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
be a parameter to be determined later.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
C
contains only gates from the following gate set

\displaystyle\mathsf{GATESET}=\{\mathsf{T},\mathsf{I},\mathsf{X},\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{CNOT},\mathsf{H},\mathsf{S},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}},\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}\}~.

(7.94)

Otherwise, we may rewrite
C
in terms of the gates from
\mathsf{GATESET}
at constant depth cost.

For each timestep
t\in[D]
of
C
, let
W_{t}
be the number of qubits.
We will have
m_{t}=\lceil W_{t}/k_{i}\rceil+3=\Theta(W_{t}/k_{i})
registers of
k_{i}
qubits.
Arbitrarily
46
46

46

This can be computed greedily.
make an injective assignment of the quantum inputs (or output in the case of
\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}
) of the gates of
C
at timestep
t
to the qubit coordinates
[k_{i}]\times[m_{t}]
indexing the qubits of the
m_{t}
registers.
We restrict the assignment such that when one of
\{\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}},\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}\}
is assigned to a register, no other operations (including
\mathsf{I}
) are assigned to that register.
This assignment also induces an assignment of the outputs.

For any permutation of
N
qubits, there exists an efficiently computable depth-
2
circuit of
\mathsf{SWAP}
gates that implements it.
We now construct a new circuit
C^{\prime}
that implements the following steps for every timestep
t\in[D]
of
C
:

1.

Implement the permutation that routes the outputs of gates at timestep
t-1
of
C
to the appropriate location for timestep
t
. Note that there are “holes” in the register coordinates
[m_{t-1}]
corresponding to blocks containing qubits that are acted on by (
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}}
/
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}
) and in the coordinates
[m_{t}]
corresponding to blocks containing qubits that will be initialized by an
\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}
.

2.

Execute all gates of
C
at time
t
on the qubits with the targets given by the previous assignment to qubit coordinates.
When a qubit of the block is acted on by one of
\{\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}\}
perform the operation on all qubits of the block (i.e.
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}}(A)
or
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A)
).
Likewise perform the
\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A)
to initialize a block when there is a qubit assigned to that block originiating from
\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}
.
For any qubit that does not correspond to a qubit of
C
, execute
\mathsf{I}
.

After replacing the
\{\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}},\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}\}
operations supported on blocks with
\{\mathsf{INIT}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A),\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{X}}(A),\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}(A)\}
, every operation in
C^{\prime}
is an operation for which there is a corresponding gadget from
Section

7.3
.

Let
D^{\prime}
be the depth of
C^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}
its width.
Let
\ell\in[1,W^{\prime}/k_{i}]
be a parameter to be selected later that will control the maximum number of gates executed in a timestep.
The circuit
\overline{C}
will be constructed from
C^{\prime}
be executing at most
\ell
gate gadgets in each timestep.

Let
t\in[D^{\prime}]
be a timestep of
C^{\prime}
.
The operations will be executed over
\tau
steps such that no step contains more than
\ell
operations.
This can be done by first constructing a maximum-degree
k_{i}
multigraph with a vertex for each register and an edge for each gate supported on qubits contained in both registers.
An edge coloring
c
of the multigraph can be computed using at most
O(k_{i})
colors.
We can compute a new edge coloring
c^{\prime}
from
c
by subdividing each color into at most
O((W/k_{i})/\ell)
new colors such that the number of edges in each color is at most
\ell
.

c^{\prime}
has at most
O(W/\ell)
colors (see
[
NP24
, Serialization Lemma]
).
From
c^{\prime}
, a partitioning of the gates
47
47

47

Single qubit gates are executed greedily subject to the constaints.
of
C^{\prime}
in timestep
t
can be computed such that:

1.

There are at most
\ell
non-trivial gates (not in
\mathsf{I}
) per partition.

2.

In each partition, no non-trivial two gates are supported on the same register.

3.

There are
O(W/\ell)
partitions.

\overline{C}
is constructed by the following procedure: For each timestep
t
of
C^{\prime}
and for each gate
\mathsf{g}
in the partitioning associated with timestep

, use the gadget
\mathsf{GATE}_{\mathsf{g}}
(from

) to execute
\mathsf{g}
and execute
\mathsf{GATE}_{\mathsf{I}(A)}
on all other blocks.

\blacktriangleleft

Proof.

Set
\epsilon_{*}=\epsilon_{*,GATE}
from
Section

7.3

Let
m=\max_{t\in[D]}m_{t}=O(W/k_{i})
be the maximum number of registers of
C
.

\overline{C}
is composed entirely of
\mathsf{GATE}
gadgets from
Section

7.3
, so the width and depth bounds will follow.
Let
\overline{W}
and
\overline{D}
be the width and depth of
\overline{C}
, respectively.
In terms of a function
f(d_{i})=\Theta(d_{i}^{5.91}~\mathrm{polylog}(d_{i}))
, (essentially corresponding to the width overhead of
\mathsf{GATE}
) the width and depth satisfy the bounds

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=O\left(\ell~k_{i}~f(d_{i})+(m-\ell)k_{i}\right)

(7.95)

\displaystyle\overline{D}

\displaystyle=O\left(D\frac{W}{\ell}~d_{i}^{2}~\mathrm{polylog}(d_{i})\right)~.

(7.96)

Using
Section

4.1
, we can show that
\overline{C}
is a
(C,\mathcal{F})
-FT gadget where
\mathcal{F}
is the sum of the bad fault paths of all
\mathsf{GATE}
gadgets in the circuit.
There are
O(m\cdot W/\ell\cdot D)
of these.
Thus, for
x\in[0,\epsilon_{*}]
, we have the bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}{\left(\mathcal{F};~x\right)}\leq O(m\cdot W/\ell\cdot D)~\mathsf{poly}(d_{i})~e^{-\beta d_{i}}~.

(7.97)

We now set
\ell=\lceil m/f(d_{i})\rceil
and use the linear distance
k_{i}=\Theta(d_{i})
, so that these bounds become

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}{\left(\mathcal{F};~x\right)}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(WD~f(d_{i})~\mathsf{poly}(d_{i})~e^{-\beta d_{i}}\right)

(7.98)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(WD~\mathsf{poly}(d_{i})~e^{-\beta d_{i}}\right)~.

(7.99)

It follows that
d_{i}=O(\log(V)~\mathrm{polyloglog}(V))
is sufficient such that the inequality
\mathcal{W}{\left(\mathcal{F};~x\right)}\leq\epsilon
holds.
In other words, we pick
i=\Theta(\mathrm{loglog}(V))
(see
Section

7.3
), utilizing the exponential spacing of the code family.
The bounds on the width and depth follow from the parameter choices and the assumption that the circuit width satisfies
W\geq\log^{7}(V)
.

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=O\left(W+k_{i}f(d_{i})\right)

(7.100)

\displaystyle=O\left(W+\log^{6.91}(V)\mathrm{polyloglog}(V)\right)

(7.101)

\displaystyle=O\left(W\right)

(7.102)

\displaystyle\overline{D}

\displaystyle=O\left(Dk_{i}~f(d_{i})~d_{i}^{2}~\mathrm{polylog}(d_{i})\right)

(7.103)

\displaystyle=O\left(Dd_{i}^{8.91}~\mathrm{polylog}(d_{i})\right)

(7.104)

\displaystyle=O\left(D\log^{8.91}(V)~\mathrm{polyloglog}(V)\right)

(7.105)

This completes our threshold proof for a qLDPC code- and teleportation-based constant overhead fault-tolerant scheme.
∎
